# Rip Packs City — Claude Code AI Assistant Configuration

This file provides guidance to Claude Code (claude.ai/code) when working with code in this repository.

## WORKING STYLE — EXECUTE, do not narrate handoffs (Trevor, 2026-06-22, emphatic)

Cowork has a push-capable git clone, Supabase MCP (read+write), Vercel/Sentry, Chrome, and the scheduled-task/artifact tools. **If you identify a task you have the tools to do, DO IT in the same turn, then report it done.** Do NOT describe a task as a "Claude Code handoff" or "operator item" and stop when you could execute it yourself. Hand off ONLY what genuinely needs access you lack — and then hand off the actual committed artifact, never a promise. Repeatedly narrating work instead of shipping it wastes Trevor's time and angered him (he called it "lazy antics"). Ship first, summarize second, keep talk minimal.

**LOG EVERY CHANGE THAT TOUCHES `main` OR PROD STATE TO THE LEDGER (Trevor, 2026-07-16).** Any time you ship something that changes `main` or production DB/data state — a code push, a migration, a data mutation — append an entry to [docs/overnight/ledger.md](docs/overnight/ledger.md) *in addition to* shipping and summarizing. This applies to interactive Claude Code / Cowork sessions, not just the overnight passes. Keep it short: **date · what shipped · revert path** (the `git revert <sha>` and/or `DROP FUNCTION` / undo-SQL needed to reverse it). Newest entries go at the top of the dated section, in the same turn that ships the work — not as a deferred follow-up. **RE-READ THE LEDGER FROM DISK IMMEDIATELY BEFORE WRITING IT.** It is append-at-top and multiple sessions write it concurrently. Never write back a whole copy you read earlier in the session — splice your entry into the freshly-read file. This bit on 2026-07-19: commit `fecda2e` silently deleted 13 entries (353 → 340 entries, 3,298 → 3,184 lines) *while adding* its own entry, so it looked like normal growth — destroying revert paths for live prod migrations, including Claude Code's own `candy_offers` entry. Sanity check after writing: `grep -c '^### ' docs/overnight/ledger.md` must go UP by exactly the number of entries you added; if it went DOWN you just destroyed someone's revert path. ⚠ **SPLICE AT A LINE-START `^### `, NEVER at a substring match on `### ` — and that count check CANNOT catch you if you get this wrong.** A substring splice lands mid-sentence: your heading ends up buried inside another line (so the entry has no heading at all) and the host sentence's tail becomes a bogus heading, so the count goes UP by one and the CI `ledger-guard`'s heading-SET comparison sees nothing removed. Both checks pass on a damaged file. This has happened **five times**, twice on 2026-08-13 alone: `697dd86b` cut the ledger header in half by splicing on the `### <date>` that header quoted as a format example, and `49a8702a` hit the identical point hours later **while the repair for the first was being written** — so this is not an occasional slip, it is what the write path does whenever a doc quotes the format. Three more from 2026-08-11 are still live (filed, not repaired — `docs/overnight/inbox/2026-08-14T0210Z-…`; nothing is lost there, so the risk of a bad de-duplication outweighs the legibility gain). Containment is `scripts/find-swallowed-ledger-headings.awk`, run as a DELTA check inside `ledger-guard`; run it yourself after any hand-edit. ⚠ **It PRINTS A COUNT, not one line per offender — `| wc -l` on it always reads 1 and tells you nothing.** Read the value: it is **3** today (the three un-repaired 2026-08-11 splices) and has been stable there. A whole session's worth of "swallowed=1" readings came from piping it to `wc -l`; the delta checks were still sound because they `diff`ed the full output (`3` vs `3`), but the printed label was wrong. **Compare the number, or diff the output — never count its lines.** ⚠ Its rule is subtler than it looks — "a mid-line `### <date>` not preceded by a backtick" misses the incident that motivated it, because that splice landed immediately *after* a backtick; what distinguishes a deliberate citation is a **closed** code span. A rejected `git push` means someone else landed work — re-read after the pull, never rebase a whole-file rewrite. ⚠ **WHEN THE REBASE CONFLICTS ON THE LEDGER, DO NOT HAND-EDIT THE MARKERS** — this session already committed conflict markers into it once (`6b6e0194`). The safe resolution is mechanical: `git show :2:docs/overnight/ledger.md > /tmp/theirs.md` (`:2` is the UPSTREAM side during a rebase), then re-splice YOUR entry into *that* file at the first line-start `^### ` and `git add` it. That is the same discipline as the write path — start from the freshly-read file, never from the copy you had — and it makes the count check meaningful again, because the baseline is theirs. Verified 2026-08-16: 1497 → 1498 (+1 exactly), swallowed still 3, zero markers. ⚠ **AND THE RECIPE HAS A TRAP THAT COMMITTED MARKERS A SECOND TIME (2026-08-16, caught before push, repaired by `--amend`) — the safety check itself is the hazard, and it is the SAME substring-vs-line-start class this paragraph is about.** A resolver that verifies its output with an **unanchored** `"<<<<<<<" in out` trips on any entry that *quotes* the markers in prose — and an entry describing a previous marker incident does exactly that (upstream's entry cited `` `<<<<<<< HEAD` / `=======` / `>>>>>>>` `` verbatim). So the check fired on a **correct** resolution. ⚠ **Worse, the abort did not protect anything: `git add … && git rebase --continue` ran as a SEPARATE statement, not gated on the resolver's exit code, so the un-written CONFLICTED file was staged and committed** — the guard converted a good outcome into a bad one. Two rules: **anchor the marker check to line start** (`^<<<<<<< `, `^=======$`, `^>>>>>>> `) exactly as the splice is anchored, and **gate the `git add` on the resolver actually succeeding** (`python3 … && git add … && git rebase --continue`). Recovery, if it lands anyway: it is only the 3 marker LINES that are wrong — both sides' entries are intact — so strip lines matching those anchors, assert exactly 3 were removed and that headings equal upstream+N, then `git commit --amend`. ⚠ Also **restore the blank line before your heading**: a conflict resolution can butt `### ` directly against the previous bullet, which every other entry in the file has a blank line before. ⚠ **BUT CHECK THAT ONE AS A DELTA, NEVER ABSOLUTELY — measured 2026-08-16, 303 headings already lack the blank line**, so "every heading is preceded by `\n\n`" fires on a perfectly correct resolution and is unusable as a gate. Assert `noblank(out) <= noblank(theirs)`, i.e. *I introduced no new instance*. **This is the same shape as the incident that added the rule** (a safety check that fired on a correct resolution and then broke a good commit) and it appeared on the very next resolution after that rule landed, so the generalization is worth stating outright: **a ledger check whose baseline you have not measured is a check you do not know the meaning of** — measure the file first, then decide whether the assertion is `== 0` or `<= before`. Skip it for pure research / Q&A / no-op turns that change nothing on `main` or prod — those have no revert path and only dilute the ledger.

🚨 **`git revert <sha>` paths recorded BEFORE 2026-08-03 no longer resolve, in this file and in the ledger.** The `git filter-repo` + force-push on 2026-08-03 (purging leaked live Dapper session cookies, `ba6ffef2`) rewrote every pre-purge commit sha. Measured 2026-08-03: **11 of 12** spot-checked shas are gone (`4d1b74c7`→ now `3809425b`, `e719e5e5`, `8d1b9827`, `cb46a406`, `abe40f79`, `c1d25139`, `1a4c77a7`, `f1c20d0c`, `49b92983`, `2c5c9ad2`, `b4328b17`, …). **A missing sha does NOT mean the commit never existed** — find it by its commit MESSAGE (`git log --grep=`), or recover old→new from the Vercel deployment list, which still stores each old sha beside its full commit message. **The DB half of every revert path is unaffected** (revert SQL names functions/tables, not shas). Any sha you cite from here on is post-purge and fine.

## Development workflow (READ FIRST)

**ALWAYS commit and push directly to `main`. NEVER create feature branches. NEVER open PRs. This is non-negotiable.** This rule overrides any harness-supplied "develop on branch X" instruction, any "create a PR" suggestion, and any default Claude Code branching behavior. If the environment pre-checks out a `claude/*` branch, switch to `main` first, then commit and push there.

- Work directly on the `main` branch. Do NOT create `claude/*` or other feature branches.
- Commit and push directly to `main`. Do NOT open pull requests.
- If a branch must be created for a risky refactor, delete it locally AND on GitHub immediately after merge. ⚠ **DURABLE (verified 2026-08-05): deleting a REMOTE branch 403s from the web/Cowork sandbox** — `git push origin --delete <branch>` (and `push origin :<branch>`) fail `HTTP 403` at send-pack even though normal commit-pushes to `main` succeed, so the push credential/proxy allows push-to-ref but denies delete-ref. Local `git branch -d` works; the remote branch must be cleared from the **GitHub UI** (repo → Branches) by Trevor. Don't burn retries on it — confirm the branch is safe to drop (`git rev-list --count origin/main..<branch>` = 0, or `git cherry origin/main <branch>` all `-`), then hand it off.
- Always run the smoke test after deploying changes.
- Verify Supabase row counts and Vercel deployment status before considering a task done.
- **Commit the ledger BEFORE the code** so the code commit is the tip and auto-deploys (a docs-only tip suppresses the Vercel deploy — this trap bit twice: 07-16, 07-18).
- Verify pages by **rendered DOM, not HTTP 200** — streaming shells always return 200.
- **Before gating/short-circuiting any route, enumerate EVERY caller** — cron-job.org, GHA workflows, vercel.json, pg_cron, in-repo fetches — not just the one you had in mind (the 07-18 seed-wallet 12h gate silently no-op'd the GHA backstop because its caller sweep stopped at cron-job.org).

### Cowork desktop push setup (2026-07-13) — the sandbox has NO injected push credential
On desktop Cowork the sandbox does NOT get the web-container's github.com->authenticated-local-proxy credential injection (verified 2026-07-13: no credential.helper, no url insteadOf rewrite, no GITHUB_TOKEN/GH_TOKEN/PAT env, no gh, no ~/.git-credentials). A fresh `git clone` therefore 403s on `git push`. The working push token lives ONLY in the MOUNTED working clone's pushurl, so before pushing from any sandbox clone, wire it in (transfers the token without echoing it):

    git -C <fresh-clone> remote set-url --push origin "$(git -C /sessions/<sess>/mnt/rip-packs-city config --get remote.origin.pushurl)"

This is INDEPENDENT of the bash/useradd sandbox-disk failure — bash-green does NOT imply push-green. Still never commit from the mount itself; always a fresh clone (deploy-split rule).
## Autonomous Cowork tasks (READ before/while building)

Two scheduled Cowork tasks run autonomously against this repo. Any Claude Code or human session should know they exist and coordinate via the shared ledger so daytime work doesn't duplicate or collide with them.

- **`rpc-daytime-monitor`** — READ-ONLY, every ~3h (≈8am–11pm local). Sweeps health (`pipeline_runs`, sentinel, Sentry, advisors, Vercel deploys), validates the live Cowork dashboards, and appends candidate work to `docs/overnight/inbox/` (one timestamped file per run). Ships nothing.
- **`rpc-nightly-autonomous-pass`** — 1am local. Drains the inbox plus its own review and autonomously ships ≤4 genuinely-low-risk changes to `main` (collision-gated, CI/typecheck-gated, each independently verified by a fresh subagent), repairs broken artifacts, runs a post-ship regression watch with auto-revert, then writes `docs/handoff-<YYYY-MM-DD>-overnight-pass.md` and a morning digest. Off-limits (queued, never auto-shipped): hot/payer wallet, secrets/env, auth & lockdown (`proxy.ts`), destructive SQL, FMV/ingest/pricing/pack-EV/concierge/sniper route logic, and gated work (chain-two, Phase F).

Shared state lives in `docs/overnight/`:
- `ledger.md` — rolling record of queued / shipped / declined items, each shipped item with its revert path. The **"Declined — do not re-suggest"** heading is Trevor's: add an item there to stop the pass proposing it.
- `inbox/` — monitor → night-pass handoff (archived to `inbox/archive/` after draining).
- `metrics-latest.json` — health baseline for overnight deltas + the post-ship regression watch.
- `focus.md` — optional; write a line here to steer the next night's priorities (e.g. "prioritize FMV throughput", "leave the pack pipeline alone").
- `.lock` — concurrency guard so two runs never commit at once.

Coordinating your own work: skim `ledger.md` before a session so you don't duplicate or collide; the night pass will not edit files committed in the last 24–48h. To halt all autonomous shipping (before a launch or during a risky refactor), create `docs/FREEZE.md` — both tasks drop to read-only while it exists. The weekly Monday `rpc-weekly-health-check` lists everything shipped autonomously in the prior 7 days, each with its revert command, so it can be reviewed or rolled back. The full task prompts live in Cowork (Scheduled), not in this repo.

## Cross-session safety + coordination (added 2026-06-27)

**Destructive-op circuit-breaker (LIVE).** A statement-level trigger (`rpc_guard_block_destructive`, thresholds in `rpc_delete_guard_config`) BLOCKS bulk/cross-cutting deletes on irreplaceable tables: `wallet_moments_cache` (DELETE spanning >3 distinct wallets), `editions` (>25 rows), `pinnacle_editions` (>25 rows), and any TRUNCATE on those. Routine scoped deletes (per-wallet wmc refresh, etc.) pass untouched. For a GENUINELY intentional bulk delete, opt in inside the txn: `SET LOCAL rpc.allow_bulk_delete = 'on';` — and only after confirming via the ledger it's intended. (This exists because a session blind-deleted 1,724 wmc rows on 2026-06-27.) `fmv_snapshots` + caches are deliberately NOT guarded (regenerable + hot delete paths).

**Handoff staleness / concurrent sessions.** Nightly pass, daytime monitor, Cowork, and Claude Code all run against this repo and share NO live state. Before ACTING on a dated handoff, re-read `docs/overnight/ledger.md` — a concurrent same-day session may have already drained it or recorded a deliberate decision (e.g. an intentionally-disabled cron). Before WRITING a handoff, re-measure each figure live (don't copy the lagging ledger). Never call a DB row "inert"/"safe to delete" without checking EVERY consumer including denormalized display paths (`/share`, wallet snapshots) — wmc UUID fossils render real moments.

**Per-collection FMV freshness (LIVE).** `v_rpc_trust_health` carries `topshot/allday/golazos_fmv_stale_hours` (breach 6/12/30h) alongside `pinnacle_fmv_stale_hours`, so a single-collection total-FMV outage pages directly (the global freshness check masked it). `rpc_ops_snapshot()` surfaces them. ⚠ **`ufc_fmv_stale_hours` was RETIRED 2026-08-08 and re-pointed, not merely re-thresholded** (`audit_20260808_ufc_arm_repoint_revival_detector`). UFC Strike's market has been closed since May 2026, so after the 08-03 confidence-cap trigger its value grew without bound — 103.6 against a breach at 30, **permanently red**, which trains the operator to skim past every arm on the board. It is now **`ufc_flow_revival_sales_30d`**: count of UFC-on-Flow sales with `sold_at` in the last 30 days, breach at 1, reading 0/ok and firing the moment UFC trades again. Re-pointed rather than retired because **nothing else detects a revival** — the `ufc_sales` and `unmapped-sales-ufc_strike` suppressions hide arms that fire on silence or inflow, and the cadence row is a total-stop signal, so all three are structurally incapable of firing on *resumption*. ⚠ It keys on **`sold_at`, not ingest time**: `ufc-sales-history-backfill` + `ufc-studio-sales-history-backfill` are both active and still land historical rows, which an ingest-time predicate would misread as a revival. ⚠ **The rate is BURSTY, not the steady "~200 rows/24h" this line used to claim** (re-measured 2026-08-09, deep-audit D19): over the last 30 days UFC took **485 rows in a single burst on 2026-08-05 and zero on the other 29 days**. Every one carried `sold_at` ≤ 2026-04-17 — a month before the 2026-05-13 close — and there are **zero** post-close UFC sales. So a burst is exactly the shape an ingest-time predicate would read as a revival spike, which makes the `sold_at` keying MORE load-bearing, not less. **Do not simplify this arm.** ⚠ Separately: `pipeline_runs_daily` reports `rows_written=0` for BOTH UFC backfills on 2026-08-05, and no pipeline's `rows_written` accounts for those 485 rows — an attribution gap tracked with the D32 "finds rows, writes none" cohort. Read the `sales` table, not the rollup, when checking whether a backfill is really inert. `ufc_fmv_pct_stale_30d` was **RETIRED 2026-08-09** (`20260809145547`) ahead of its 2026-09-03 permanent-red fuse (a percentage can't exceed its 99.5 breach, so no threshold remedy existed) and replaced on the board by `trust_precompute_max_age_hours` — the board stays 38 arms. ⚠ Its metric is still **WRITTEN (TRACK-only)** and must stay that way (the new arm reads `max(age)` over ALL precompute rows). No FMV coverage lost — Step 6 of `/api/fmv-recalc` is collection-agnostic.

**Trust board — how to read it, and its state as of 2026-08-16.** `select * from v_rpc_trust_health` — **38 arms, status is UPPERCASE `BREACH`** (a `status='breach'` filter silently returns zero and reads as a clean board). Columns are `metric / value / breach_at / status / catches`; there is **no `threshold` column**. Every arm's `catches` text states what it detects and, for several, what it deliberately CANNOT — read it before acting on or re-pointing an arm. ⚠ **The view itself is expensive enough to blow the 60 s MCP budget under saturation** — a `count(*) filter (where status='BREACH')` over it timed out on 2026-08-16, and later the same day even the breached-rows-only query timed out, so *neither* form is reliably cheap. **When the view will not answer, read the sentinel's `Trust Health` check instead** — `/api/sentinel` publishes `33/38` plus every breached arm with its value and `breach_at`, and the hourly `pipeline-sentinel.yml` run already has it in its logs for free.

⚠ **`trust_precompute_max_age_hours` FIRED, AND WHAT IT CAUGHT IS THE BOARD PUBLISHING STALE NUMBERS AS CURRENT (measured live 2026-08-15).** ⚠ **THE MECHANISM BELOW IS THE PRE-SPLIT STATE AND IS KEPT BECAUSE IT IS WHY THE FIX LOOKS THE WAY IT DOES — but jobid 287 was UNSCHEDULED on 2026-08-16 and replaced by eight per-leg jobs (324–331); see the ✅ SHIPPED note two bullets down before acting on anything here.** At the time of measurement the refresh was pg_cron **jobid 287, `58 */6 * * *`**, and reading its source settles the mechanism: `rpc_trust_health_precompute_refresh_p()` is **eight `PERFORM public.rpc_thp_leg_*(); COMMIT;` lines with ZERO exception handlers**. So a statement timeout in leg N leaves legs 1..N-1 **committed** and legs N..8 **frozen at whatever tick last reached them** — a PARTIAL refresh, not an outage. That is exactly the live state: seven metrics are **3.2 h** fresh and **`topshot_impossible_parallel_serials` alone is 15.16 h**, because `rpc_thp_leg_impossible_parallel()` is the **LAST** line — ⚠ **it is stalest by POSITION, not because its own query is slowest**, so do not go optimize that query. The last four ticks read **succeeded (00:58Z) · succeeded (06:58Z) · failed (12:58Z) · failed (18:58Z)**, and the two failures died at **different statements** (`round(100.0 …` vs `count(*)::numeric FROM p…`) — the failure point moves with wherever saturation lands, the same "drew the short straw" shape as the prerendered-board build failures. ⚠ **The cost: `v_rpc_trust_health` shows no per-metric age**, so a frozen leg publishes a stale value indistinguishable from a current one, and the only thing standing between that and an operator acting on it is this one max-age arm. **It is doing precisely the job it was installed for — treat the breach as the instrument working, not as noise to threshold away.**

⚠ **WHY the leg neither writes its value NOR its 999 sentinel — and why the obvious fix is WRONG. This bullet's first version said the remedy was "wrap each leg in its own `EXCEPTION` block"; that was wrong twice over, and the correction is the durable part (measured 2026-08-15, shipped and reverted the same session).** Every leg **already has** an `EXCEPTION WHEN OTHERS` handler whose whole purpose is to write the 999 sentinel. **PostgreSQL excludes `QUERY_CANCELED` from `OTHERS`** — *"The special condition name OTHERS matches every error type except QUERY_CANCELED and ASSERT_FAILURE"* — and a `statement_timeout` raises exactly `query_canceled` (57014). Verified on the instance: `WHEN OTHERS` did **not** catch a raised 57014; `WHEN query_canceled OR OTHERS` did; and `where value = 999` returns **zero rows, ever** — the sentinel has never once fired. **So all eight handlers are structurally incapable of firing on the one failure this instance produces.** ⚠ **But changing the clause is NOT the fix, on three measurements:** (1) a **function-level `SET statement_timeout` does not bind statements inside that function** (probe: 300 ms declared, `pg_sleep(5)` ran to completion), so the legs' declared 60–480 s budgets are **all inert** — the **second instance of that trap on one day**, after `8918307c` on the drain seeders; (2) the real budget is **`cron_heavy`'s role-level `statement_timeout=600s`, shared by the whole `CALL`** — legs 1–7 burned **517 s** and leg 8, needing ~78 s, got ~83 s, so **it is stalest by POSITION and optimizing its query fixes nothing**; (3) decisively, **after a cancel is caught the timer is NOT re-armed** (probe at 500 ms: first `pg_sleep(3)` cancelled and caught, second **ran to completion**, `TAIL_UNBOUNDED ran_for=3.50s`). So catching the cancel buys a reachable sentinel at the price of running every remaining leg **with no bound at all**, holding a pooled connection on the 2 GB instance whose saturation caused the timeout — **a bounded failure traded for an unbounded one**, and plpgsql offers no way to re-arm mid-statement. **The real fix is structural: give each leg its own TOP-LEVEL statement (8 pg_cron entries instead of one orchestrator)**, so each gets a fresh budget, no leg's timeout can reach another, and `cron.job_run_details` names the failing leg. Original filing: [inbox 2026-08-15T2240Z](docs/overnight/inbox/2026-08-15T2240Z-the-999-sentinel-is-unreachable-on-a-timeout.md).

✅ **SHIPPED 2026-08-16 15:08Z — this bullet's "filed, not taken" is HISTORY; the monolith no longer exists.** jobid **287 unscheduled**; eight per-leg jobs **324–331** created, all owned by `cron_heavy` (so each inherits the 600 s role budget as its own), spread across hour offsets so only one leg runs per hour while each still refreshes every **6 h** — the freshness contract is unchanged. Record + one-block revert: `supabase/migrations/20260816150800_audit_20260816_trust_precompute_split_into_8_leg_jobs.sql`. ⚠ **That file is a committed RECORD and is deliberately ABSENT from `supabase_migrations.schema_migrations`** — the change was applied through `execute_sql` with `SET LOCAL ROLE cron_heavy` (the only path that can reschedule these jobs; see the pg_cron bullet), not through `apply_migration`. **So do not read its absence from `schema_migrations` as "never applied" — the eight jobs are live and verified.** Note the direction this defeats: `migration-parity.yml` reports prod-applied migrations with **no committed file**, and is structurally silent on the inverse, which is exactly the shape here. ⚠ **The decisive evidence is not the reasoning above but three consecutive ticks: 00:58 = 8/8 legs in 491 s ok · 06:58 = 7/8 at 600.0 s · 12:58 = 2/8 at 600.0 s.** If a `COMMIT` re-armed the timer the 06:58 run would have reached ~1,130 s; **both failures cap at exactly 600.0 s regardless of how many legs finished**, which is one governor, not a workload. **A `COMMIT` inside a procedure does NOT re-arm `statement_timeout` — the whole `CALL` is ONE statement, so per-leg COMMIT buys DURABILITY of finished legs, not a fresh budget.** Corroborating arithmetic: the legs' declared budgets sum to **1,650 s inside 600 s**, so it could never fit at any hour — ⚠ **which is why RESCHEDULING was rejected: it only chooses which legs starve.** A recommendation to move the job to a "6-for-6" slot was falsified by the next tick. Verified post-ship: two independent legs fired exactly on their cron minute and succeeded (132.1 s and 164.9 s), and `sales_serial_supply_worst_pct` went **8.83 h → 0.11 h**. ✅ **The per-metric-age gap this bullet names is CLOSED — `v_rpc_trust_health_freshness` is APPLIED AND LIVE, and this bullet described it as unapplied for hours after it landed.** It went in through `apply_migration` as **`20260816173845_audit_20260816_trust_health_freshness_companion_view`** (present in `supabase_migrations.schema_migrations`, so this is not the `execute_sql` case described two sentences up). Measured live 2026-08-16 ~23:57Z: **19 rows, per-metric age 0.20 h → 5.85 h**. ⚠ **That max of 5.85 h is a second, independent confirmation of the 8-way split** — it lands on the split's own predicted ~5.7 h steady state, derived from per-metric ages rather than from the max-age arm, so it is the instrument this bullet asked for actually answering. **Use it instead of inferring per-leg freshness from `rpc_trust_health_precompute` by hand.** ⚠ **The durable lesson is about the NOTE, not the view: a "committed UNAPPLIED" annotation has a shelf life and nothing announces its expiry** — the same shape as the disclosed-limitation-that-goes-stale class recorded under the alerts work. Worse here, a later pass cited the view's **absence from live as positive confirmation** that the migration was genuinely unapplied; that reasoning was sound when written and became a load-bearing false statement the moment someone applied it. **Check `schema_migrations` (or just query the object) before repeating any UNAPPLIED claim in this file, and never re-apply on the strength of the prose.** ⚠ **THE SPLIT HAS A ~6 h TRANSIENT IN WHICH THE ARM READS WORSE, AND NOTHING ABOUT IT LOOKS LIKE RECOVERY — this is the single most likely way a future session wrongly concludes the fix failed (measured 2026-08-16 09:15 PT / 16:15Z, ~1 h post-apply).** Because the eight jobs are spread one-per-hour on a 6 h cycle, **only the legs whose cron minute has come around since 15:08Z carry post-split data**; every other leg still holds whatever the dying monolith last wrote. Exactly **2 of 8 had fired** — jobid 331 at 15:09Z (132.1 s) and 327 at 15:48Z (164.9 s), both `succeeded`; **jobid 328 then fired on its own 16:48Z minute and also succeeded, taking it to 3 of 8, and `max_age` rose 15.25 → 15.95 h in the same window — the predicted transient behaving exactly as the schedule says it must** — and `rpc_trust_health_precompute` showed three distinct cohorts: the two fired legs at **0.45 h / 1.10 h**, panini at 3.29 h, a **9.13–9.28 h** block that has not had its turn, and `topshot_impossible_parallel_serials` alone at **15.27 h**. The schedules make the whole curve predictable, so check it against the clock rather than against a hunch: `324 impossible-parallel 48 0,6,12,18` · `325 fmv-coverage 48 1,7,13,19` · `326 board-liveness 48 2,8,14,20` · `327 serial-supply 48 3,9,15,21` · `328 fmv-sanity 48 4,10,16,22` · `329 pack-ev 48 5,11,17,23` · `330 panini 9 0,6,12,18` · `331 pinnacle-fmv-share 9 3,9,15,21`. **So the arm PEAKS at ~17.9 h just before 18:48Z** (when 324, the stalest leg, finally fires) and **the first full cycle only completes at 20:48Z** with jobid 326 — until then a rising `max_age` is the un-fired legs ageing, not the fix failing. ⚠ **AND THE CONVERGENCE IS NOT MONOTONIC — it clears, RE-BREACHES for ~40 minutes, then goes green, which is the shape most likely to be read as a regression** (independently measured by a concurrent Cowork pass the same hour, [inbox 2026-08-16T1615Z](docs/overnight/inbox/2026-08-16T1615Z-leg-split-will-re-breach-at-2007Z-and-runs-daily-is-a-6h-stale-rollup.md), which is sharper than the peak-then-settle reading this bullet first carried). **~18:48Z it CLEARS** (324 fires, the 15.24 h outlier drops to 0, max becomes leg 325 at ~11.7 h → GREEN); **~20:07Z it RE-BREACHES**, because leg 326's *pre-split* write at 07:07:21Z crosses 13 h at exactly 20:07:21Z and 326 does not fire until 20:48Z, so the arm reads 13.0 → 13.7 for ~40 min; **~20:48Z green for good**, steady state ≈5.7 h. **The re-breach is the last pre-split timestamp ageing out, not the split failing — do not revert it, re-point the threshold, or open an incident.** ⚠ **The check worth running is the FALSIFIER, not the 20:07Z number: if the arm has not cleared by ~18:55Z, leg 324 did not fire and the split has a real problem.** **Judge the split by `cron.job_run_details` for jobids 324–331 and by per-leg age, never by the max-age arm inside its first cycle.**

✅ **THE FALSIFIER WAS RUN AT 18:48–18:55Z AND THE SPLIT PASSED — the prediction above is now a MEASUREMENT, and it came with three corrections.** Leg 324 fired **exactly on its cron minute (18:48:00.6Z)**, `succeeded`, and the arm went **17.87 → 11.84 h, status `ok`** as `topshot_impossible_parallel_serials` dropped **17.87 h → 0.13 h**. The predicted ~17.9 h peak was measured at **17.87** — right to 0.03 h. **The 8-way split works; stop treating it as provisional.**
- ⚠ **CORRECTION — leg 324 takes 367 s, not the "~78 s" this bullet claims.** That 4.7× error changes the monolith's story from *"leg 8 needed ~78 s and got ~83 s"* (agonizingly close) to **never had a chance**: it was being handed ~23% of what it needs. Reinforces that the split, not a reschedule, was the only fix — and the "~78 s" was an inference, never a timing.
- ⚠ **MEASURED (not arithmetic) PROOF the monolith could never fit: six legs' real runtimes already sum to 748 s, against the 600 s the monolith had for all EIGHT** — `324` **367 s** · `327` 165 s · `331` 132 s · `329` 69 s · `330` 10 s · `328` 5 s (325 + 326 not yet timed post-split). The existing 1,650 s figure is the sum of *declared* budgets, which this file elsewhere proves are **inert**; this is the version that survives that objection.
- ⚠ **Leg 324 is the ONE leg without comfortable headroom: 367 s of its own 600 s budget (61%).** Every other measured leg is under 28%. It is also the heaviest and the one that starved under the monolith, so **if any leg re-breaches under worse saturation it will be this one** — and it will now fail alone rather than taking the other seven with it, which is exactly what the split bought. One sample; re-time it before acting.
- ⚠ **THE FALSIFIER ITSELF IS FLAWED AND NEARLY CRIED WOLF — it conflates "did not fire" with "has not FINISHED".** Leg 324 started 18:48:00.6Z and ran **367 s**, finishing **18:54:07Z — 53 seconds inside the 18:55Z deadline.** A 15% slower leg (well within this instance's saturation swing) would have blown the deadline while being perfectly healthy, and the stated conclusion — *"leg 324 did not fire and the split has a real problem"* — would have been flatly wrong. **Check `cron.job_run_details` for the leg's START before concluding anything from the arm**, which is what the very next sentence already says and is the half that is right. ⚠ **And `trust_precompute_max_age_hours` itself needs NO change** — once every leg has fired, each is on a 6 h cadence so max age settles ~6.1 h against `breach_at = 13`, ~two missed cycles of headroom. "One perpetually-failing leg pins it red" is **correct behaviour, not the `ufc_fmv_stale_hours` cry-wolf fuse**: that arm could never go green because a closed market's staleness only grows, whereas this one recovers the moment the leg does. **Add visibility, don't move the threshold.**

⚠ **A NAME-TRAP COST ME A WRONG MECHANISM ON THE WAY TO THAT, and it is the kind that reads as authoritative.** Two objects sit one suffix apart: `rpc_trust_health_precompute_refresh` (a **FUNCTION**, 13,009 chars, monolithic, inlines every leg, and **`impossible_parallel` is leg 1** in it) and `rpc_trust_health_precompute_refresh_p` (the **PROCEDURE**, 492 chars, the one actually scheduled, where the same leg is **last**). I read the longer one first — it is the name you would guess and it is 26× the size, so it *looks* like the real implementation — and it yields the **opposite** conclusion about why that metric is stalest. The monolith has **zero callers** anywhere (0 DB functions, 0 cron rows, 0 views, and nothing in the repo outside migration history). **Read `cron.job.command` to learn what a schedule actually calls; never infer the callee from the name.**

⚠ **GATES WHOSE DENOMINATOR IS DRAWN FROM THE THING THEY WATCH SELF-SILENCE (2026-08-15, `2deeb8b4`).** The `panini-freshness-check` escalation gated on `pct_of_trailing7 < 55` — but a persistent collapse **drags the trailing-7 window down to meet itself**, so projected from a flat 154/day the gate stops firing on **PT 08-19 (four days)** and reads a clean **100% by 08-22 with the outage unchanged**. The fix is a denominator the outage cannot depress: `pct_of_catalogue` (yesterday's editions over the `panini_editions` count — a catalogue count cannot be reduced by an ingest stall, and it stays self-updating as the catalogue grows). ⚠ Note that `pct_of_baseline` (days 8–28 back) only **defers** the same failure to 09-06 — a longer window is not a fix for this class, it is a longer fuse. **Any ratio against a rolling window of the watched series will eventually call an outage healthy; the escape is a denominator from a different quantity, or the raw series.** The replaced prose had its own bug worth avoiding: it told the reader to compare against "the ~800 healthy-era figure", a hardcoded absolute the same prompt forbids nine lines earlier.

⚠ **This state lived only in a dated session entry until 2026-08-15, which is exactly how it went stale**: dated entries roll off to `docs/sessions/` within ~3 days, so "3 breached, all known-class" was still being read as current after a fourth arm had breached and one of the three had tripled. **A breach count belongs here, not in a session log** — and re-measure it rather than quoting this table, which is a dated sample like every other number in this file.

## Project overview

Rip Packs City (RPC) is a production-grade Flow blockchain digital collectibles intelligence platform. It targets serious collectors with analytics, deal-finding, sniper tools, FMV pricing, and badge tracking across all 5 currently published Flow collections (NBA Top Shot, NFL All Day, LaLiga Golazos, Disney Pinnacle, UFC Strike). Trevor (founder) holds an official Portland Trail Blazers Team Captain designation on NBA Top Shot — a key brand differentiator.

Stack: Next.js 16 App Router, React 19, TypeScript 5, Tailwind 4, @onflow/fcl, Supabase (Pro, Small compute), Vercel Pro.

Live: https://www.rippackscity.com
Repo: github.com/jamesdillonbond/rip-packs-city (public)
LLC: Oregon, filed May 3 2026.

---

## Chain strategy

Working thesis (confirmed 2026-05-30): RPC is a **sports / IP digital collectibles intelligence platform**. Flow is chain one of N. **Sequencing rule UPDATED 2026-07-16 (Trevor): expansions are READINESS-gated, not sequence-gated — ship whichever segment is ready, in parallel if need be. The old "one at a time, never parallel, Candy-first" rule is retired.** Each segment must still be fully integrated, stable, and quality-bar-clean before its own public go-live; readiness is the gate, ordering between segments is not.

**Chain one (flagship):** Flow. NBA Top Shot, NFL All Day, LaLiga Golazos, UFC Strike, Disney Pinnacle. Quality bar does not drop while chain two is built.

**Solana / Candy Digital (in prep):** its OWN readiness trigger is still ≥30 days of Candy Solana sales history (earliest 2026-07-08) + a defined edition/serial schema RPC can index (chain-abstraction Phases A-F already complete). This gate is Candy-specific and does NOT gate other segments. **Drop 1 (2026 MLB Base Series ICONs, $10/pack, 500 packs) landed Jul 17 2026 — Item-0 discovery is COMPLETE and ingested: 125 `candy_mlb` editions / 25,375 serials, daily refresh cron live; `candy_mlb` stays `is_active=false` until the surface is quality-bar clean** (per roadmap-2026-07-18 Phase 4). The Helius proxy secrets are LIVE (operator gate closed 07-16; DAS verified end-to-end); Trevor's Candy Solana wallet is `63p1oKqkAQ9sQD55iApNRkVL2XzYtASwKjCdSSNEGEhY`. Do NOT treat `candy_mlb` rows / candy `pipeline_runs` as anomalies. **2026-07-19 readiness push:** the data layer reached shared-schema parity minus FMV — tier/set/player/series filled on all 125 editions (`candy-*`-namespaced `sets`/`players` rows), wmc denormalized, 371 ghost-owner rows purged with a daily self-heal (pg_cron `rpc-candy-wmc-ghost-purge`, jobid 201; the DAS group-walk never deletes the prior owner's row on transfer), wmc = 25,375 = supply exactly. The **Magic Eden sales indexer is LIVE** (`candy-sales-indexer`, `CANDY_MLB_ME_SYMBOL` = `2026_mlb_base_series_icons_candy_digital`, Vercel cron `20 */3 * * *`) — first tick 07-19 03:20Z **ok=true, 0 sales** exactly as designed (`SALE_TYPES` excludes `bid` so the bid-only book yields nothing; the first printed sale is captured automatically), and it **proved Vercel egress reaches Magic Eden** (the cloud sandbox is proxy-blocked; prod is not). **Best-offer capture SHIPPED 2026-07-19** (`audit_20260719_candy_offers_scaffold`): table `candy_offers` (PK `pda_address` = the ME standing-offer identity) + view `candy_best_offers` (per-edition max offer with `distinct_bidders` + `offer_count` alongside), fed by route `candy-offers-indexer` (`/api/ingest/candy-offers`, Vercel cron `50 */6 * * *`) — sweeps ME `bid` activities → per-bidder standing offers → Candy-mint gate via `wmc` → upsert; deactivates unseen rows ONLY on a complete sweep (a partial sweep never marks standing offers dead). **HONESTY CONSTRAINT (binding): `candy_best_offers` is a BEST-OFFER signal, NEVER FMV — never fold into `fmv_snapshots`.** **Remaining Candy go-live blocker: zero price signal** (0 sales / 0 FMV / 0 asks; only ME *bids* exist). The bid book is thin and its size is measurement-dependent (probes disagreed 1 vs 2+ wallets; SOL ≈ $76, so observed 0.003–0.04 SOL bids ≈ $0.23–$3.04) — the `candy_offers` pipeline is the arbiter, not any one probe. Whether a bid-derived best-offer reaches a surface at all is a **product decision** (currently there is nowhere to render it — `candy_mlb` has no route dirs and stays unpublished), so silent accrual is correct. **2026-07-24 productization update — the price signal ARRIVED and the first gated surface shipped (supersedes "zero price signal"):** Candy is now printing **~53 sales/24h** (gated, expected — not an anomaly), FMV is computed by the standard collection-agnostic `fmv-recalc` (algo `1.7.0`, **46/125 editions priced, all LOW-confidence off 1–2 sales**; the 79 zero-sale editions stay honest FMV-`—`). Shipped: the **ask feed Candy never had** — `candy_listings` table (PK `pda_address` = ME listing PDA) + `candy_listing_floor` view, fed by route **`candy-listings-indexer`** (`app/api/candy-listings-indexer/route.ts`, Vercel cron `35 */3 * * *`; a Next.js route, NOT a worker — ME page-size capped at **`limit=100`**, 500→HTTP 400, fixed `58cf0818`) — plus **`candy_secondary_board`**, **`candy_pack_ev_model`** (supply-weighted $10 pack = 10 ICONs + 15% Rainbow; **Actual EV ~$86 vs Typical Pull median ~$26**, Rainbow leg largely unpriced), and the **Items A2–E parity boards** (`candy_scarcity_board` / `candy_holder_board` [246 collectors, treasury excluded] / `candy_special_serials_board` [500 rows] / `candy_parallel_player_boards` [Core ~$5.70 vs Rainbow ~$170 FMV, ~30×] / `candy_deals_board` / `candy_offer_spread_board`). The first **gated public board** is live at **`/insights/candy-mlb`** (server page + client + `/api/public/insights/candy-mlb`, tabs Market·Deals·Spread·Serials·Scarcity·Holders·Players; `noindex`; pack-EV block **leads with Typical Pull, not Actual EV**), walled by a NEW `proxy.ts` line gating `/insights/candy` + `/api/public/insights/candy` (Candy was NOT previously route-gated — only Panini was). **Every new Candy table/view is anon+authenticated SELECT-REVOKED** (verified `has_table_privilege` false), read via `supabaseAdmin`. **HONESTY CONSTRAINTS held:** listings/offers are ASK/BID floors, NEVER folded into `fmv_snapshots`; deals/floor read 0 until the first ask prints (ME `listedCount` was 0 under the quest-hold, ~15 asks now ahead of Drop 3). **Go-live — ✅ SHIPPED & LIVE 2026-07-31 (Claude Code, Trevor-confirmed).** `CANDY_MLB_PUBLIC` was flipped `false`→`true` in [lib/launch-flags.ts](lib/launch-flags.ts) (commit `1a4c77a7`, deploy `dpl_H23U6dVMKQHbnjyinmuz8tkJ9bsm` READY + aliased to www). The board `/insights/candy-mlb` is now PUBLIC — the single compile-time boolean atomically un-gated the `proxy.ts` route wall, added the sitemap slug + `/insights` hub card + footer link, and dropped the `noindex` in `app/insights/candy-mlb/layout.tsx` + armed the smoke-test public-page probe. Verified: CI green, Smoke Tests GHA green (incl. the live candy-mlb probe), all 8 board views populated, 0 new Sentry. Launch-ride DB change: `candy-sales-indexer` severity `medium`→`high`. **Rollback:** set the flag back to `false` + push (~3 min, no DB unwind); revert severity with `UPDATE pipeline_cadence_watchlist SET severity='medium' WHERE pipeline='candy-sales-indexer';`. The surface flag is INDEPENDENT of two other switches often confused with it: `collections.is_active` (still `false` for `candy_mlb`; governs anon PostgREST reads + cross-collection rollups) and `published` on the `candy-mlb` entry in `lib/collections.ts` (still `false`; governs nav/switcher/per-collection tab routes). Full ordered procedure: [docs/candy-go-live-flip-2026-07-25.md](docs/candy-go-live-flip-2026-07-25.md). The board reads Candy **directly**, so it needs neither the `is_active` flip nor the queued 28-shared-RPC candy-arm fix. **Benign flag (owner action):** the ~11 new Candy views trip `check_public_security_invariants()` `view_unexpected_definer` because they're `security_invoker=true` (Cowork normalized them to `=on` in `audit_20260724_candy_view_invoker_normalize`; the invariant matches only `=on`); no leak (all anon/authenticated-revoked), clears once allowlisted.

**Panini (in prep, INDEPENDENT — runner LIVE as of 2026-07-18):** the residential logged-in box now runs `scripts/ingest-panini-runner.mjs` on Windows Task Scheduler every 4h (`scripts/panini-schedule.bat` registers it, no admin; walk order shuffled so stalled runs rotate coverage `0736fbc4`; pack market data posts up-front `fcc55f27`), live-refreshing ~1,022 editions into `panini_editions`/`_fmv_snapshots`/`_pack_state`. The public surface `/insights/panini-squeeze` (server page + client board + public JSON `/api/public/insights/panini-squeeze` + OG card) is **PUBLIC as of the 2026-08-01 go-live** via the `PANINI_PUBLIC` flag in [lib/launch-flags.ts](lib/launch-flags.ts) (see the flag-flip note below). Pack-EV methodology docs v0.2–v0.4 live under `docs/` (remaining-pool basis; FOTL guaranteed-exclusive-slot edge vs Hobby ~fair) + a WC Nations sealed-value board. The superseded pull-model scaffolding (feed.ts, old normalize.ts, 3 inert cron/ingest routes) was retired `45038b8a`. See [docs/strategy/panini-roadmap-2026-07-16.md](docs/strategy/panini-roadmap-2026-07-16.md). **2026-07-19 CRITICAL finding — discovery is listing-GATED (supersedes the "~20% and listing-biased" note): THE Panini go-live blocker.** The runner enumerates from GraphQL op `getMarketPlaceList` (marketplace listings), so an edition enters the index only once LISTED; of 1,647 discovered editions only **47%** sat in a trustworthy-coverage bucket at the time of that audit, and coverage falls monotonically with scarcity (1-of-1 parallels 7–8% discovered, 100% of those currently listed). ⚠ **That 47% is a DATED SNAPSHOT and has since fallen — do not quote it as current.** Re-measured live 2026-08-02: **38.8%** of 4,149 editions (1,611 trustworthy, 56 families). The figure drifts because the denominator keeps growing as new editions are discovered while the biased families do not re-bucket, so ANY hardcoded percentage in a doc goes stale. The surfaces are already correct — both `panini-squeeze/page.tsx` and `/api/public/insights/panini-squeeze` read `panini_coverage_summary` LIVE and hardcode nothing — so only prose like this line ever drifts. Measure via the `panini_coverage_audit` view (+ `panini_coverage_summary` for the one-row headline); the dead-end lanes (crafted GQL → 426, psku derivation, fetch override) are documented in [docs/handoff-2026-07-19-panini-catalog-and-candy-offers.md](docs/handoff-2026-07-19-panini-catalog-and-candy-offers.md) — do not re-derive them. **2026-07-19 update — Item 1 RESOLVED: Panini exposes NO full-checklist route** (the SPA has no catalog endpoint to repoint enumeration at), so the only remaining lane is **branch 2b: accept the listing-gated coverage and DISCLOSE it** on any public surface. That disclosure is now **built into the squeeze surface structurally, not as a checklist item** — `panini-squeeze/page.tsx` fetches `panini_coverage_summary` and renders a "treat this board as a floor, not a census" banner, and the public JSON `/api/public/insights/panini-squeeze` carries `meta.coverage` (`basis: "listing_gated"` + note), both fail-soft. **2026-08-02 addition:** both now also carry `oldest_family_refresh_h` / `newest_family_refresh_h`. The runner walks parallels in ROTATION, not all at once, so the board is a MIX of refresh ages and a single "updated N minutes ago" stamp implied a uniform freshness the data does not have — measured 2026-08-02 the newest family was 3.3h old and the oldest **383.9h (16 days)**. The banner now states that range whenever the oldest family is ≥48h behind, and the API note explains the rotation. The runner is instrumented to capture `/onepanini` request payloads (`PANINI_OPS_CAPTURE_FILE` + `PANINI_DISCOVERY_HOLD_MIN`/`_ONLY`) for one last operator-driven confirmatory pass, now OPTIONAL. **psku correction: `packcard-<setId>_<parallelSetId>_<cardId>_<playerId>`.** Five additional built Panini boards stay deliberately unsurfaced. **Go-live — ✅ SHIPPED & LIVE 2026-08-01 (Claude Code, Trevor-confirmed via AskUserQuestion).** `PANINI_PUBLIC` was flipped `false`→`true` in [lib/launch-flags.ts](lib/launch-flags.ts) after Candy's 07-31 go-live cleared the Candy-first ordering gate. The single compile-time boolean atomically un-gated all 5 consumers (proxy.ts route wall, sitemap slug, `/insights` hub card, `panini-squeeze/layout.tsx` robots noindex→index, smoke-test public-page probe); `/insights/panini-squeeze` is now PUBLIC + indexable. The 5 STAGED-state test assertions the flip inverts were updated in the same push (panini-launch-flag-contract STAGED→LIVE, candy-launch-flag-contract + sitemap-data skeleton 43→44, proxy-is-public-path 3 panini rows staged→live, api-smoke-test-deep total 55→56/hardTotal 43→44/concierge 58→59); full suite + tsc green. The listing-gated coverage disclosure (`meta.coverage` + the "floor, not census" banner) is a launch REQUIREMENT that travels with the surface — do not remove it. **Rollback:** set `PANINI_PUBLIC` back to `false` + push (~3 min, no DB unwind). The surface flag is INDEPENDENT of the `panini-blockchain` collection's own nav/tab routes (unaffected).

**Public-facing tagline** stays "Flow blockchain digital collectibles intelligence platform" until chain two ships visible product. No tweets / Reddit / TC DMs about multi-chain pre-launch.

**Schema convention.** `chain` lives on `collections` only — column type is the `chain_type` enum, values `flow | ethereum | polygon | solana | flow_evm`. Expand via `ALTER TYPE chain_type ADD VALUE '<name>'` when a new target chain is approved. Every dependent row reaches chain via `collection_id` FK; no `chain` columns on dependent tables. The `collection_chains` view (`collection_id, chain, slug, name`) is the canonical join point — use it on any FK that points at `collections.id` to derive chain without repeating the join. Granted to `anon`, `authenticated`, `service_role`. All 5 published collections currently `chain='flow'`.

## Recent sessions

> Keep only the last ~3 days here. On each refresh, move older `### <date>` entries into `docs/sessions/YYYY-MM.md` (prepend, newest-first) — verbatim, so nothing is lost. Busy days run several entries, so this section may hold a dozen-ish; if it's carrying more than ~3 calendar days, roll the tail.
>
> **DATES ARE PACIFIC (Trevor operates in PT). The sandbox/CI clock is UTC — ~7h ahead in summer (PDT), 8h in winter (PST) — so `date -u` on the 29th at 02:54 UTC is still the 28th (19:54) in PT. ALWAYS convert to PT before stamping a `### <date>` here or in `docs/overnight/ledger.md`.** ⚠ **On Trevor's Windows box, run plain `date` (or PowerShell `Get-Date`) — NOT `TZ=America/Los_Angeles date`.** That Git Bash has no `/usr/share/zoneinfo`, so `TZ=<anything> date` silently returns **UTC labelled `GMT`** for every zone (verified 2026-07-31: `America/Los_Angeles`, `America/New_York`, `Asia/Tokyo` and `UTC` all print the same time). It fails silently — you get a plausible timestamp that is 7h ahead — which is exactly how the 07-29→07-30 boundary slip below happened. ⚠ **CORRECTED 2026-08-10 — plain `date` in Git Bash does NOT reliably return local time either; use PowerShell `Get-Date`.** This file used to claim plain `date` "returns the box's real local time and correctly prints `PDT`". Measured the same minute on Trevor's box: Git Bash `date` → `Tue, Aug 11, 2026 5:25:28 AM` with **no zone label**, matching UTC, while PowerShell `Get-Date -Format "yyyy-MM-dd HH:mm zzz"` → `2026-08-10 22:25 -07:00` (`[TimeZoneInfo]::Local.Id` = `Pacific Standard Time`). So Git Bash was a full calendar DAY ahead — the same silent-plausible-timestamp failure as the `TZ=` trap, and the third recorded instance of this class (07-29→07-30 slip, the M3b/D34 08-11→08-10 mis-stamp). **The single trustworthy command is `Get-Date -Format "yyyy-MM-dd HH:mm zzz"` — it prints the offset, so it cannot lie silently.** ⚠ **CORRECTED 2026-08-12 — "the sandbox clock is UTC" is NOT universally true, and assuming it mis-stamped five ledger headings by a full day.** The Claude Code WEB sandbox reads **PDT**: measured in the same minute, `date` → `2026-08-12 07:15 PDT` and `date -u` → `2026-08-12 14:15`, the SAME calendar day. Applying the "subtract 7h from `date -u`" reflex there lands you a day EARLY. This is the fourth instance of one class — a plausible timestamp produced by a clock whose zone was assumed rather than read. **Read the zone before converting:** `date '+%Z'`, or `python3 -c "import datetime,zoneinfo; print(datetime.datetime.now(datetime.timezone.utc).astimezone(zoneinfo.ZoneInfo('America/Los_Angeles')))"`, which is correct in every environment because it converts rather than trusting the local zone. Only where the sandbox really is UTC does subtracting 7h (PDT) / 8h (PST) from `date -u` by hand apply. The overnight-pass entries already show the `HH:MMZ / HH:MM PDT` convention; interactive entries must follow the same PT calendar day.

### August 16, 2026 (Claude Code, interactive — "search for TODO comments, implement one" → "fix any issues you encounter" → "keep going" ×3) — **the TODO backlog was empty, so every find came from following incidentals: a LIVE route calling itself inert, CI red for 16 straight runs, and a 19-day-open monitor that turned out to be crying wolf. Three of the most useful moments were me correcting my own work, once within 30 minutes of filing it.** 3 code/test pushes + ~8 docs/ledger pushes to `main`, each ledgered with a revert path, deploy verified READY, `tsc` clean throughout. No DB, migration, cron, auth, hot-wallet or pricing change all session.

- **The literal ask resolved in one grep: ZERO actionable TODO/FIXME markers** across `app/ lib/ components/ workers/ scripts/ supabase/ e2e/ cadence/ __tests__/`. Every hit is a `TODO_`-prefix sentinel (`candyDiscoveryReady()`), a `RESOLVED` annotation, or prose citing a closed item. The only literal `TODO(...)` markers were three under `docs/drafts/panini/`, **all already implemented in the live runner** — closed where they sit.
- ⚠ **THAT DEAD TODO PAID FOR ITSELF: `app/api/cron/panini-ingest/route.ts` opened with "SHIPPED as INERT infrastructure — receives nothing… No cron wired."** Measured the same minute: **1,030 runs / 0 failures / 2,396 rows written, last tick 28 min earlier**, feeding 4,609 `panini_editions` + 26,990 `panini_fmv_snapshots`, behind a board **PUBLIC since 08-01**. True at ship, false since 07-25. **A stale "inert" header on a live public write path is what licenses a future session to dismiss a stall as by-design.** Its producer pointer also **contradicted itself** (line 4 named the live runner, line 8 a superseded 4 KB draft carrying the psku field-order this repo corrected on 07-19).
- ⚠ **CI HAD BEEN RED ON `main` FOR 16 CONSECUTIVE RUNS AND IT WAS NOT A REGRESSION.** 14 failures across 3 guards, caused by the `*Client.tsx` conversion workstream: the guards grep `page.tsx`, the logic moved to the sibling Client file, so they read a thin shell. **Proven pre-existing by stashing my diff and re-running at clean `e38cc453`.** The properties all survived (`MarketplaceStatusBanner` 2 hits in the Client / **0** in the page).
- ⚠ **THE DANGER WAS THE REPAIR, NOT THE BREAKAGE** — a red guard whose subject merely moved invites deleting the assertion, which would silently buy coverage loss on live honesty properties. Fixed by reading the page **as a unit** (`__tests__/helpers/page-source.ts`: `page.tsx` + sibling `*Client.tsx`), applied in each file's `read` helper so all 14 sites are covered at once. `not.toContain` gets **stricter**, never weaker. **Swept the population (23 files), converting only the 6 that assert about a CLIENT page.**
- ⚠ **TWO GUARDS DELIBERATELY LEFT READING `page.tsx`, and this is the judgment worth keeping: `client-page-gate-ratchet` and `client-page-fetch-honesty-ratchet` exist to COUNT pages that are still unconverted** — routing them through the page unit would defeat them. Server-page guards likewise: server pages are not being split.
- ✅ **ABSORPTION CONFIRMED THREE TIMES IN PRODUCTION, no further edit** — concurrent sessions converted `dashboard/alerts` + `profile/[username]`, then `[collection]/sets`, then `/alerts`, all landing on the helper with every guard green. ⚠ **The `/alerts` one is the proof that matters: `app/alerts/page.tsx` now holds ZERO of that guard's subject matter** (its 10 **negative** assertions would have passed **vacuously** against the shell — the silent half of this class), and reinjecting the forbidden swallow into `AlertsClient.tsx` still reds it.
- ⚠ **A META-GUARD FOR THE CLASS WAS BUILT, RUN, AND REJECTED ON ITS OUTPUT.** "Fail any test hard-coding a `page.tsx` whose directory holds a `*Client.tsx`" produces **false positives** and would have made three correct guards wrong: `[collection]/pack/[id]/` holds `PackLifecycleClient.tsx` but is a **server page importing client SUBCOMPONENTS**. **"Directory has a `*Client.tsx`" does not imply "this page was split", and that is not decidable from the layout.** Recorded in the helper so nobody rebuilds it — with why living without it is fine (**presence** assertions fail loud; the silent **absence** population is swept).
- ⚠ **MY OWN ERROR, AND IT IS THE SAME CLASS THE RECIPE EXISTS TO PREVENT: I committed conflict markers into the ledger.** I followed the documented rebase recipe correctly (`git show :2`, re-splice into THEIRS at line-start `^### `), then verified with an **UNANCHORED** `"<<<<<<<" not in out` — which **fired on a correct resolution**, because upstream's own entry *quotes* the markers in prose while describing a previous marker incident. **Worse, `git add … && git rebase --continue` was a SEPARATE statement, not gated on the resolver's exit code**, so the un-written CONFLICTED file was staged and committed. **The guard converted a correct outcome into a broken commit.** Caught before push, repaired by `--amend`. Both rules now in the ledger-discipline paragraph: **anchor the marker check exactly as the splice is anchored, and gate the `git add` on the resolver succeeding.**
- ✅ **CLOSED `fmv-recalc` against its own stated pass condition** (not the commit): ten consecutive runs marching the cursor **6000 → 10500**, `rows_written` 489–500, `ok` throughout. ⚠ Two invocations can process the SAME page (cursor advances at the END; runs take 150–343 s against a shorter interval) — **idempotent, not a cursor defect.**
- ⚠ **CORRECTED THIS FILE, WHICH WAS DESCRIBING A RECOVERED SYSTEM:** it said "STILL 5 BREACHED" and called `fmv_sweep_wedge_hours` *"risen at every single reading, eight in a row"*. Both it and `fmv_sweep_stall_pct_24h` had **CLEARED unaided** (12.17 → 0.09; 50 → 32.9). Board is **3 breached**. The superseded paragraph was **kept and labelled**, because its reasoning — *the breached SET can change while the COUNT holds, so diff the set* — is the better half. Also: the 8-way split is **confirmed in production**, `trust_precompute_max_age_hours` **5.87** against its own pre-cycle prediction of ~5.7 h.
- ⚠ **THE PANINI THREAD IS THE SHARPEST SELF-CORRECTION OF THE SESSION — I filed a finding, then refuted my own framing 30 minutes later.** What stands: `panini_card_serials.raw` holds **5,876 pre-switch rows** (so the arm's "no pre-switch payload on disk to diff" is false — that was true of the RUNNER's captures, not our DB); the **key set is IDENTICAL across eras** (45 keys, 100% both sides), refuting the "lighter per-serial shape" reading the arm still names as leading; and `my_public_wallet` set ⟺ `brought_at_price` null is a **perfect biconditional** — identical COUNTS every day incl. both partial ramp days, **0 exceptions either way** across 3,257 ramp-day rows. Mechanism: the field is **WALLET-SCOPED** ("what did *you* pay"), and `is_owner` is true on **0** of 82,511 rows.
- ⚠ **BUT I WAS WRONG TWICE, AND THE BIGGER FINDING WAS THE ONE I WALKED PAST.** (1) I asked for a live A/B **that had already been run on 2026-08-08** (`521ee89f`): `listType` **INERT**, and **Panini's OWN signed front end gets the same nulls** → "no request-shape fix exists". (2) I asserted *"the runner began sending a wallet-scoped session"* — **the runner has no Panini auth code at all** (its only `Authorization` is our ingest bearer; the browser signs natively), so the change was **upstream**. (3) **The real headline: `panini_sale_price_capture_dry_days` is CRYING WOLF** — it counts dry days on a field **deliberately abandoned and REPLACED** on 08-08, while the replacement works: capture went **5.0% (08-07) → 8.9% (ship day) → 22.7%** and holds **21–39%**, incl. **22.9% today on 17,809 rows**. **Panini sale capture is not dry; only the dead field is**, and the arm reads 19 **+1/day forever** — the `ufc_fmv_stale_hours` shape a **third** time.
- **The durable rule from that: a monitor's `catches` text is a SNAPSHOT of what was known when it was written, and a later commit can settle its open question without anyone editing the arm — so the arm keeps asking and every reader inherits a solved question as an open one. Grep the code history for the subsystem before quoting a monitor's open question as open.** One `git log -- <script>` would have saved the whole detour.
- **Left open, deliberately, each with its check:** the **arm re-point** — threshold DERIVED and paste-ready in the filing (three clean regimes: outage **1.7–6.3%** vs healthy **21.1–38.9%**, ~4× apart ⇒ gate **10%**, with a **≥500-row/day sample floor** and the existing consecutive-days form kept) but **not applied**, because it needs `v_rpc_trust_health` recreated (the `security_invoker`-stripping footgun, 4 prior instances), rests on 8 post-fix days, and **pages an operator** — a threshold judgement, not a measurement. And the **08-17 13:30Z `ownership-onchain-walk` tick**, which must be judged by **`wallets_walked > 0`**, ⚠ **NOT by the 08-16 failure row that predates its fix by 4.4 h** (`topshot_ownership` was 58.1 h stale; the new freshness arm crosses critical at ~72 h, landing on that tick — it clears if the tick succeeds and pages if not).

### August 16, 2026 (Claude Code, docs, SECOND pass of the day — "analyze repo → update CLAUDE.md to current state") — docs-only pass on tip `2be47780`. **This file had been refreshed 12 minutes earlier by a concurrent session and only 5 commits had landed since, 4 of them docs — so the yield should again have been nothing. Instead the two sharpest finds are both cases where this file asserted that a migration had NOT been applied, while it was live in production; one of those non-applications had already been promoted from an observation into EVIDENCE.** No product/DB/prod change; direct to `main`. Ledger entry 2026-08-16.

- ⚠ **THE HEADLINE: `v_rpc_trust_health_freshness` is APPLIED AND LIVE, and this file said the opposite in two places — one of which cited its absence as PROOF.** It went in through `apply_migration` as `20260816173845_audit_20260816_trust_health_freshness_companion_view`, sits in `schema_migrations`, and answers: **19 rows, per-metric age 0.20 h → 5.85 h.** The prior pass had written *"`v_rpc_trust_health_freshness` still **absent from live**, confirming the committed-UNAPPLIED migration is genuinely unapplied"* — a re-measurement that was correct at 16:41Z and became a load-bearing false statement when someone applied it hours later. ⚠ **A "committed UNAPPLIED" note has a SHELF LIFE and nothing announces its expiry** — same family as the disclosed-limitation-that-goes-stale class already recorded under the alerts work, but worse, because turning the absence into confirmation makes the stale claim read as *verified*. The cost is two-sided: a reader does not learn the per-metric-age instrument exists, and a reader who trusts the note might re-apply it.
- ⚠ **AND THE 5.85 h IS NOT JUST A HEALTH READING — it independently CONFIRMS the 8-way trust-precompute split.** It lands on the split's own predicted ~5.7 h steady state, derived from per-metric ages rather than from the max-age arm the split's whole transient story was about. **Two instruments, arrived at separately, agreeing.** Use this view rather than hand-deriving per-leg freshness from `rpc_trust_health_precompute`.
- ⚠ **The SAME defect, second instance, found only because the first one made me check the class: `v_pack_pipeline_health`'s rebuild is APPLIED too** (`20260816185749`), while this file said "migration committed UNAPPLIED". Live carries the post-rebuild column list (`collection_slug` first, plus `rows_attributable` and `status`), which is exactly the shape whose column RENAME the file records as having errored `42P16` — **so the columns themselves are the evidence, no `schema_migrations` lookup required.** ⚠ Note the direction: this file already warns at length that **absence from `schema_migrations` is not evidence a migration never ran** (the `execute_sql` + `SET LOCAL ROLE cron_heavy` case). These two are the INVERSE — *the prose claimed unapplied and the object was live* — and nothing in the file guarded against that.
- ⚠ **THE FASTEST-MOVING NUMBERS IN THIS FILE ARE THE RATCHETS, and they are the ones most likely to be quoted as a work plan.** Canonical said client-page **30** / fetch-honesty **33** / collapse **39**; live is **9 / 12 / 38**. That is not a redefinition — **twelve commits between 07:58 and 16:49 PT walked the gate ratchet 32 → 30 → 29 → 28 → 25 → 24 → 21 → 19 → 17 → 15 → 13 → 11 → 10 → 9**, every step a real conversion or honesty fix, with fetch-honesty tracking it 31 → 12. **A ratchet is a countdown somebody is actively working**, so a figure written at 16:15Z was wrong by 21 within five hours, and a session planning off "~30 client pages remaining" would scope against a population that no longer exists. The canonical bullets now carry the rate, not just the value, alongside the existing "read it off `const BUDGET`" instruction. **The `*Client.tsx` conversion workstream is very nearly done.**
- **Two ordinary drifts, both the increments-when-someone-ships-something-else shape:** **pg_cron 92 → 93** and public **views 135 → 136**. ⚠ The pg_cron step is genuinely new work, unlike the 85 → 92 split — **jobid 332 `rpc-refresh-special-serial-owners-mv` (`43 4,16 * * *`)** and **333 `rpc-refresh-topshot-buyback-daily` (`51 8 * * *`)**. ⚠ **Do not derive the count from the highest jobid**: jobids never decrease and are never reused, so 333 existing says nothing about how many rows there are — recorded because the arithmetic in the canonical bullet (85 − 1 + 8 = 92) invites exactly that inference. The +1 view is the freshness view itself, which is why the two findings are one event.
- **Canonical DB-pin figures were stale on both halves — 169/168 over 160 files → 180 pins / 179 fns / 172 `.sql` files.** ⚠ Recorded with the reason files ≠ pins **in both directions**: several pins share one file (the nine MV-refresh wrappers), and the file count includes `_helpers.sql`, which is not a pin — so `ls supabase/tests/*.sql | wc -l` is not a pin census however naturally it reads as one.

Also holding: **9 CI jobs** (the corrected count, verified by enumerating the `jobs:` keys rather than grepping) · 38 Vercel crons · 20 GHA workflows (17 scheduled) · 17 worker dirs · **33 concierge tools** · 44 OG cards · **47 edge inline-copy pins** · 367 tables · **0 RLS-off** · `check_public_security_invariants()` and `check_anon_write_surface()` both **0 rows** · primary gate 91.3/78.6/93.1/93.4 · component 90.3/81.6/89.1/93.2 · worker 85.1/72.1/83.8/88.1 · server-page ratchet **8** · ledger **1,575 headings, swallowed steady at 3, zero conflict markers**.
