- ⚠ **Three of my own claims needed correcting mid-thread, each caught by measurement rather than review:** an invalid-param sweep that reported **9 broken pipelines** which were logging normally (my parser counted keys nested in `p_extra`); a heartbeat I nearly shipped passing `p_finished_at`, a parameter that does not exist, which would have made it *never write while looking correct*; and a session of `swallowed=1` readings that were counting output lines of a script that prints a count (**3**). **The pattern is the same one this thread kept finding in the codebase, and it does not spare the person looking for it.**
- **Left OPEN deliberately, each with the lane it needs:** R5's pipeline deploy (an edge secret — operator-only, and reading it would leak it into a transcript); R9's two pricing questions (Trevor); R6/R8/R14 (DB-migration lane, where a concurrent session traced the shared saturation to **three heavy pg_cron jobs colliding at `:13`** — ⚠ **that attribution was REFUTED 2026-08-16 (minute 13 now holds one hourly job; overlap tracks job DURATION, not start minute), though the saturation it was reaching for is real and still the common cause behind the fmv-recalc kills, the insights-cache failures, the entity-page pool timeouts and the R17 backfill skips**).

### August 15, 2026 (Claude Code, interactive — "search the codebase for TODO comments, implement one" → "keep going" ×5) — **the TODO backlog really is drained (0 actionable markers outside `docs/`), so every item came from measuring something; the two best finds were a FILED RECOMMENDATION that measurement refuted, and a guard whose own derivation made the production path untestable.** 5 code pushes to `main` + 4 docs pushes, each ledgered with a revert path, every deploy checked PER COMMIT and READY, `tsc` + primary gate green on each (11,750 → **11,803**). No DB, migration, cron, auth/`proxy.ts`, hot-wallet, or FMV/pricing-MATH change all session — the DB and FMV lanes were concurrent sessions' and I stayed out of them, rebasing 4 times.

- ⚠ **THE HEADLINE: a filed fix was measured and REFUTED, and shipping it would have been worse than the defect.** The inbox recommended re-pointing the tautological Pinnacle FMV drift guard at `pinnacle_fmv_history` as "a genuine cross-source check". It is not a source at all — it is written by an `AFTER INSERT/UPDATE` **trigger on `pinnacle_catalog`**, so the comparison is the same tautology one hop removed, *except* where the trigger drops rows, which it does for **776 renders**. The "proper fix" would have replaced a guard that never fires with one that pages immediately, on an unrelated defect. **Second time in three days a recommendation died on measurement** (the `websearch_to_tsquery`/stemming pair was the other) — a plausible mechanism reads as a conclusion, and the only defence is to measure before shipping someone else's fix.
- ⚠ **That investigation found a REAL defect by chasing an arithmetic gap.** `NOW()` is transaction-stable, the Pinnacle recalc writes each render twice per transaction with the same `fmv_computed_at`, and the trigger's `ON CONFLICT (render_id, computed_at) DO NOTHING` silently discards the ASK_ONLY revision. Split is exact — HIGH/MEDIUM/LOW/STALE **0 differ**, ASK_ONLY **776 of 1,053**, max **$2,974.47** — so a Pinnacle edition page plots a series whose latest point is not the FMV shown on the same page. Filed (migration on FMV capture, not autonomous). The guard was **RETIRED** rather than re-pointed; what prevents the leak is structural (ask and FMV come off the same row) and is now pinned by a SOURCE guard, because a runtime probe can only observe the consequence — which is exactly how it degenerated.
- ⚠ **A mechanism's own derivation decides what it can observe — THREE new instances this session.** (1) `withDeadline` bounds an attempt two ways and they produced two different errors; **in production the abort wins**, so the shape that escaped had none of the `RPC_TIMEOUT` properties the module advertises — and **no test could catch it, because every mock implements `.rpc` with no `.abortSignal`**, so all of them take the guard branch by construction and the guard branch was already correct. (2) The metadata honesty layer was the only one of five with **no guard**, and both pack pages' `generateMetadata` discarded the `ok` their own page BODIES use. (3) My own new guard was **silently broken and still passed its own coverage assertion** — a whole-file `\([\s\S]*?\)` backtracks across function boundaries, so it discovered **1** fetcher instead of 54.
- ⚠ **An instrument's REASONING can be sound while its PREMISE is false.** `/api/cron/refresh-insights-cache` ruled `ok = okCount > 0` because "a single board timing out is EXPECTED". Measured over 869 ticks: `deals` failed **59.5%** of ticks (longest streak **34 ≈ 2h50m**), and `okCount > 0` was satisfied almost entirely by candy-mlb at 95.6% — **the healthiest board vouching for the other four**. A tick's outcome structurally cannot express a cumulative quantity. Fixed by reading snapshot AGE, with the 2 h ceiling derived from the streak distribution (only 4 of 414 streaks reached it) rather than chosen.
- **Two questions CLOSED with arithmetic rather than left open.** Serializing the board warms is impossible: the six views' **mean** costs sum to **59,960 ms** against a `maxDuration` of **60 s**. And those views are **STARVED, not slow** — every max is ~29.x s (the 30 s ceiling) while means are 10–12 s, and `panini_squeeze_board` fails 51% of ticks on a **3.4 s mean**; each failure burns a full 30 s of DB time for nothing, ~90 s per tick, so the refresher feeds the saturation it exists to survive.
- **Also shipped:** the `MomentDetailModal` bio wiring, closing the half-feature this file had flagged — deliberately a **lazy fetch on open** rather than the filed "change both monolith queries", since the table holds hundreds of rows and a collector opens one.
- ⚠ **Process lessons paid for.** A `git checkout <file>` used to unwind a mutation test **silently reverted an entire retirement** — caught only by re-grepping, not by trusting the command; use a backup copy on a file you are mid-edit on. And `list_deployments` with a `since` in the FUTURE returns zero results, which reads exactly like "no deploy was triggered".
- **Left OPEN and filed, all needing lanes I stayed out of:** the `pinnacle_fmv_history` ASK_ONLY drop (migration); the entity-page **pool-acquire** family — `NEXTJS-1Z` and four siblings die at our 45 s bound while Postgres self-bounds at 30 s and the heaviest real call maxes at 13.6 s, so **a query-plan fix cannot close them** and two attempts already have not; and the six insights views needing to come under 30 s, with three candidate concurrency fixes each recorded WITH the reason it is a gamble.

### August 15, 2026 (Claude Code, docs — "analyze repo → update CLAUDE.md to current state") — docs-only pass on tip `8f59749b`. **Every repo-side count held; every drift was on the LIVE side — and the sharpest find was a contradiction already sitting inside this file, where one Sentry issue ID had been given two different diagnoses in two bullets a day apart, and neither noticed the other.** No product/DB/prod change; direct to `main`. Ledger entry 2026-08-15.

Read live, the issue is titled **`pack detail bundle unavailable: TimeoutError: The operation was aborted due to timeout`**, culprit `GET /[collection]/pack/dist/[distId]`, **86 users and still firing** — an abort on a slow read, and **Sentry groups by title, so `PGRST002` events were never in that issue at all.** The tell was already here: the Aug-14 entry independently calls `NEXTJS-1Z` *"pack detail bundle unavailable, 40 users"*. **Both sessions found a real defect and then reached for the biggest ID on the board to attach it to.** The migration→500s causal measurement stands on its own and is kept; only the attribution is corrected. **An issue ID is not a diagnosis — read the title and culprit, which is one query.**
- ⚠ **A second documented trap was stated too broadly and would INVERT a verdict.** "`count(*)` over a `check_*` function returns 1 when CLEAN" holds only for the jsonb-array ones (`check_secdef_anon_exec_drift()`: count **1**, array length **0**). The SETOF invariants are the opposite — `check_public_security_invariants()` and `check_anon_write_surface()` both return **ZERO rows** when clean, so `count(*)=0` *is* health there. Measured all three in one query; **check the return type before interpreting the count.**
- ⚠ **The trust board drifted where this file could not see it — because its only record was a DATED entry.** "38 arms, 3 breached, all known-class" was still being read as current with **4** breached: `public_board_slow_count` had **tripled 3 → 9** (worst four all Candy; `candy_pack_market` **128.8 s for a ONE-ROW result** behind a board public since 07-31), and **`fmv_sweep_wedge_hours` is a NEW breach outside the known-class set**. Now a canonical table, with the reading gotchas that cost me two queries: status is UPPERCASE **`BREACH`** (a `='breach'` filter returns zero and reads as a clean board) and there is **no `threshold` column**.
- ⚠ **Following that new arm found the largest genuinely-new operational finding: `fmv-recalc` is degraded and has been all month.** Chronic **~8–13%/day** failures since ≥08-08, then a step change on **08-14** where runs/day **HALVED (93 → 41)** and rows written fell **45,054 → 10,684** and have not recovered (18,720 on 08-15). **Two regressions, easy to conflate** — and the halved run count is the more alarming half, since it means runs were not *started*, which no failure-rate story explains. Every recorded `last_error` is saturation-class, converging with the entity-page and insights-cache findings on one root cause: **concurrent connections against a 2 GB instance.**
- ⚠ **And that arm has a trap I nearly reported as a wedge:** `fmv-recalc`'s `extra` carries **no `cursor_before`/`cursor_after` keys on any row**, so a cursor-advance query returns NULLs meaning *the key is absent*, not *the cursor did not move*. Read `rows_written` from `pipeline_runs_daily`, which is also the only place the pre-08-12 history survives the 73 h prune.
- **Four code commits carried durable lessons documented nowhere canonical**, all now folded into the sections that own them rather than left in a session log: the `withDeadline` **two-shapes** bug (`b0cebf90` — the abort path produced an error with neither SQLSTATE nor `RPC_TIMEOUT`, and **no test could reach it because every mock lacks `.abortSignal`**; the board displayed the split for weeks, `-26` showing one shape and `-1Z/-23/-22/-20` the other); the insights-cache `ok = okCount > 0` blind spot (`f077d51f` — **869 ticks of perfect health** while `deals` failed 59.5% of them, the healthiest board vouching for the rest); the `/api/edition-floor` **unauthenticated service-role DELETE** (`8f59749b` — under a `proxy.ts` comment asserting "No writes, no user data", and **`check_anon_write_surface()` is blind to it by construction** because it tests the anon DB role, not a route holding the service-role key); and the **sixth** failure-renders-as-data instance, on a page D11 had already hardened.
- **Verified CURRENT, which is the useful negative result:** 38 Vercel crons · 8 CI jobs · 20 GHA workflows (17 scheduled) · 17 worker dirs · **125 DB pins over 124 fns** · **32 concierge tools** · 8 collections / 5 published · `editions` **36 columns** · 44 OG cards (39 direct + 5 via `entity-card`) · 11 quirk kinds · primary 91.2/78.3/93.0/93.35 · component 88.5/79.4/88.2/91.6 · ratchets **35** / **33** · pg_cron **85** · 367 tables / 134 views / **0 RLS-off** · description coverage **9,128/13,208 (69.1%)** and bio 9,082/9,082/9,037 — both **unchanged since 08-14**, worth stating because this file warns any such figure is a dated sample.
- ⚠ **Concurrent-session collision, and it landed on the SAME two bullets — resolved by merging, not by keeping both.** A parallel session documented the `withDeadline` and warm-cron lessons while this pass was writing them; the push was rejected and the rebase conflicted on exactly those hunks. **Concatenating would have produced two bullets saying the same thing — the precise failure this pass had just corrected in `NEXTJS-1Z`.** So each was merged on merit: **theirs won the warm-cron serialization question outright**, because they closed it with arithmetic (the six backing views' mean costs sum to **59,960 ms** against a 60 s `maxDuration`, so the boards are **STARVED, not slow**, and each failure burns 30 s for nothing) where I had only bounded it at 5 × 30 s; my `BOARD_SNAPSHOT_STALE_CEILING_MS` derivation, the cry-wolf link and the user-impact statement were grafted in. Mine was kept for `withDeadline` (it names `-26` as the *counter*-shape and links the guard-scope class). **Their unique entity-page bullet independently corroborates this pass's `NEXTJS-1Z` correction** — it splits the entity timeouts into Postgres's 30 s `statement timeout` family and our own 45 s abort family, and puts `NEXTJS-1Z` (86 users) in the second. Two sessions reaching the same conclusion from different directions is the useful part; two bullets asserting it separately would not have been.

### August 14, 2026 (Claude Code, interactive — continuation of the CX/concierge thread: "keep going" ×3 → "make sure everything is addressed") — **the day's two biggest wins came from measuring a fix BEFORE shipping it and from asking what was eating the IO budget instead of optimising the page that hurt.** 6 pushes to `main`, each ledgered with a revert path; `tsc` clean; primary 11,750 + component 1,670 green. 3 prod DB changes (1 migration, 1 `cron.alter_job`, 1 index drop), each verified live.

- ✅ **NARRATIVE SEARCH IS FIXED, on the THIRD diagnosis — and the first two were mine.** The defect was never the ranking: `rpc_search_catalog` required EVERY query token (`LIKE ALL`), so one word the prose never uses annihilated the query. `lillard buzzer beater` returned **0 rows** while `lillard buzzer` returned the moment at rank 6. Fixed (`audit_20260814_rpc_search_catalog_token_coverage`): a **3+-token query may miss ONE token**, scored by a `(tok_hit / v_n) * 0.60` coverage term so a full match still outranks a partial; 1- and 2-token queries still require every word (relaxing there degrades `lillard buzzer` into every Lillard moment). All three failures fixed, all three working ranks and every entity query unmoved. Canonical detail is in the `/api/search` bullet.
- ⚠ **BOTH "proper" fixes I had recommended in the filings would have FAILED, and I only know because I measured them instead of shipping them.** `websearch_to_tsquery` does **not** give OR-semantics — it emits `'game' & 'winner'`, keeping the exact AND that was the defect — and English stemming does **not** relate **winner↔winning** or **buzzer↔beater**, so FTS would have missed the precise two moments that motivated the whole thread.
- ⚠ **Part of the improvement was NOT the fix, and saying so mattered.** Description coverage moved **44.6% → 69.1%** overnight on the catalog cron. The fix is a **no-op for 1- and 2-token queries by construction**, so every better bare-phrase result is coverage growth. **Any narrative measurement older than a day is a dated sample.**
- ⚠ **I went to ship my own filed fix for the top user-facing error and it was a REGRESSION — the planner agreed with me and was wrong.** Full detail now lives in the `fmv_snapshots` section; the short version is that "add `collection_id` to reach the covering index" does exactly what it promises and still loses, because the covering index is 113 MB with 15.6k lifetime scans against a 61 MB one with 38.9M, and its `Index Only Scan` still did **1,292 heap fetches of 1,531**. What shipped instead was `and fs.computed_at <= now()` — runtime partition pruning of two provably EMPTY partitions, **9,131 → 6,308 buffers on the same hot index**, semantically a no-op. ⚠ **It does NOT close `NEXTJS-1Z`**: the same query is 30 ms warm, 5.1 s evicted, 12.9 s while FMV recalc dirties pages. The mechanism is buffer thrash on a 2 GB instance.
- ⚠ **The #1 disk reader on the whole instance was a backfill asking the wrong question — 113 GB, and the fix was one cron argument.** Found by ranking `pg_stat_statements`, not by chasing the pages that were timing out. Live ticks **2:13 → 3.5 s**. Also dropped a 72 MB index unused in 63 days. Both are written up under "General rules", including the eleven unused indexes I deliberately did NOT drop and why.
- ⚠ **A tidy hypothesis nearly cost a working job.** The obvious story for that backfill was zombie rows that could never satisfy its join; a 200-row sample said **200/200 would be updated**. It had real work left and was simply being asked the wrong question. **A cheap sample beats a plausible mechanism.**
- **The player-bio columns landed and populated on their first cron tick** — 9,082 / 9,082 / 9,037 of 13,208 canonical Top Shot editions — so the `Play.stats` field placement was right and the 422 trap was dodged. ⚠ **But three of `classifySerial`'s eleven quirk kinds were structurally unreachable in production** because both callers passed only `circulationCount`. The concierge half is now wired (fail-soft, mutation-proven); `MomentDetailModal` is not. **Wiring a capability to its data is a separate act from shipping either one** — the second instance of that in two days.
- **Also verified rather than assumed:** the catalog walk's `sets_faulted: 11` reproduced **byte-identically** on the next night's run (9,396 / 541 / 258 / 11), which is strong evidence the fault set is STABLE, not flaky — filed with the cross-run query, since `pipeline_runs` retention is ~73 h and the `errors_sample` set ids vanish with it. And `refresh_wmc_fmv_changed` (112 GB, #2 reader) was characterized as **not a defect** so nobody spends a session on it.
- **Left for an operator, unchanged:** All Day prose/bio is still **0%** behind a WAF 403 that is almost certainly the one-line `ALLDAY_PROXY_URL` → `/allday-consumer` env change ([inbox 2026-08-14T0500Z](docs/overnight/inbox/2026-08-14T0500Z-allday-waf-403-is-a-one-line-env-fix.md)). An env change needs a REAL rebuild — `vercel.json`'s `ignoreCommand` skips a docs-only or empty commit.

### August 14, 2026 (Claude Code, interactive — "improvements to the RPC profile page and trophy case, and how trophy cases get shared to Twitter/X" → "default the RPC username to their Top Shot username, keep going on the rest" → "keep going" ×6) — **the ask was flair and pretty link previews; the measurement said it was a FUNNEL problem — 16 of 20 collectors had no public profile at all, so there was nothing to flair and nothing to share.** ~20 pushes to `main`, each ledgered with a revert path, both gates green on every push, every deploy checked PER COMMIT.

- ⚠ **THE FRAMING FIND, from counting before building.** `profile_bio` held 20 rows but only **4 with a username** — and *a username is what creates `/profile/<username>` at all*. Every downstream feature (flair, sharing, follows, the OG card) was dead by default for 80% of signups. Closed forward (handle defaults from the Dapper/Top Shot username) and backward (16 collectors backfilled, Trevor-authorized): **4/20 → 20/20**. Canonical detail is in the Route-structure bullet.
- ⚠ **A false `$0` sat in the unfurl of every profile for ~2 months** — `generateMetadata` published "Portfolio: $0 FMV across 0 moments" on any failed read, and self-fetched its own API to get there. **Unnoticed because metadata output is invisible in the browser and only ever seen in someone else's timeline.** That is the FIFTH layer of the failed-read-renders-as-an-answer class and is now a row in the honesty table, along with the `openGraph`/`twitter` shallow-merge gotcha.
- **Shipped, in the order the thread found them:** all **43** OG cards branded + cached behind one loader; **trophy notes**, the flair field that already existed in schema, RPC and renderer with no UI to write it (0 of 16 trophies had one); a **shareable `/profile/<u>/trophy-case`** page + card; `/profile/edit` live preview; the unequip path; duplicate-pin prevention; and the picker **confirm step** that names the trophy it will replace.
- ⚠ **The rewards shop would sell a cosmetic the app cannot draw, and take the credits** — found by *assessing* a product decision ("should cosmetics be data-driven?") rather than answering it. Detail in the `lib/cosmetics.ts` bullet.
- ⚠ **THREE investigations ended in findings I could not ship, and filing them was the deliverable.** (1) The board's **top user-facing error by user count** (`pack detail bundle unavailable`, 40 users) is NOT a missing bound — deadline and error-vs-absent split were both already there; the RPC is 18,951 ms cold vs 406 ms warm. ⚠ **A later session took my filed fix, MEASURED it, and it was a regression** (the index it unlocks is 1.85× larger and effectively cold; its "Index Only Scan" does 1,292 heap fetches because delete-then-insert keeps pages non-all-visible) — **and my filing's "prunes the other partitions" claim was wrong.** Filing a hypothesis AS a hypothesis is what made that catch possible. (2) The **Pinnacle FMV drift guard compares `pinnacle_catalog` to ITSELF** — deal rows and comparison rows are the same table and columns, so it cannot fail for the reason it exists; it went tautological when the FMV source moved off `pinnacle_editions` and nothing announced it because nothing failed. **54 pages, none of them possible.** (3) **Two schedulers run the same wmc FMV propagation** over the same rows — **92 lock timeouts** beside 108 statement timeouts — and the heavier caller is invisible to the `pipeline_runs` logging added specifically to make that observable (logged max 34.9 s vs 364 s observed live).
- ⚠ **Method, and I paid for each.** A guard that fires is not a guard that is right: the drift-guard investigation killed **five** hypotheses, including one that looked conclusive (397 of 2,561 catalog rows really do have untrimmed `set_name`, and the leak strings show it) before reading `tripleKey` and finding it already trims. A `pg_stat_activity` query matching on a function name **matches itself** — that became a wrong "2 concurrent instances, confirmed live". And **`get_deployment.state` lags**: `BUILDING` across four polls over ~45 min on a deploy that was live and aliased, and I produced two confident wrong diagnoses before checking a second field — *a real adverse condition was underway, which is exactly what made the false story plausible*.
- ⚠ **Two of my own tests were vacuous and mutation caught both**: a font test that passed with fonts stubbed to null (the loader fails soft, so "a PNG came out" asserts nothing — assert a BYTE DIFFERENCE), and a flair test that varied fonts *and* flair, so the difference could have come from the typeface. **Hold everything but the one variable.**
- **Left open deliberately:** per-slab styles (needs a `slab_style` column plus a design decision on the finishes), and the drift-guard retire/re-point call. `tierAccent` has no case for Pinnacle's `STANDARD` tier — consistent grey, so a missing design decision rather than a bug.

## Route structure

Feature pages live at `app/(collections)/[collection]/`. The layout at that level provides header, nav, and ticker — pages must NOT include standalone headers.

The `[collection]` dynamic segment serves all 5 published collections: NBA Top Shot, NFL All Day, LaLiga Golazos, Disney Pinnacle, UFC Strike. Each collection's page set is its `pages: [...]` array in `lib/collections.ts`, but since the **2026-07-18 IA reorg** the TOP BAR renders `tabBarPages()` = `pages` minus `TAB_BAR_HIDDEN_PAGES` (`packs`, `pack-sniper`, `hot-floors`, `challenges`) — those stay registered pages so every gate, capability check, and collection-switch keeps working; only the tab bar hides them. Per-collection `pages` (verified 2026-07-18):

- **All 5 published:** `overview`, `collection`, `sniper`, `analytics`.
- **`market` + `packs`:** all except UFC (Pinnacle gained both in the IA reorg).
- **`sets`:** all except Pinnacle.
- **`pack-sniper`:** Top Shot + AllDay only.
- **`challenges` + `hot-floors` + `play`:** Top Shot only.

**How the folded pages are reached (IA reorg conventions):** the **Moments | Packs sub-toggle** (`components/collection/PackSubNav.tsx`) mounts under the Collection / Market / Sniper tabs and is URL-param driven — `?section=packs`, NOT nested routes, so sub-views stay deep-linkable and the parent tab keeps highlighting (the market page already owns `?view=` for grid/table, which is why the toggle uses `?section=`). "Moments" is relabeled "Pins" for Pinnacle. Top Shot's `play` tab is the **Play hub** (`play/` route dir) fronting Challenges, Fast Break, and Road to the Ring. `components/collection/FeatureTabGate.tsx` (used by `market/layout.tsx` + `sets/layout.tsx`) gates those routes for collections that don't list the page.

**Market vs Sniper split (Trevor, 2026-07-18): Market is EDITION-level (one row per edition; AllDay via RPC `get_allday_market_editions`; Pinnacle via the render-keyed live-listings source reusing `computePinnacleSniperFeed`), Sniper is SERIAL-level (individual listings).** Market defaults to Price ascending.

Top Shot's Fast Break and RTR (Road to the Ring) game features live at `fast-break/` + `road-to-the-ring/` route dirs — still not registry tabs themselves (they appear in no `pages` array; the `play` hub links to them). Entity/detail routes under `[collection]` (also not tabs): `edition`, `moment`, `set`, `series`, `player`, `team`, `pack`, `profile`. There is NO standalone `badges` tab — the page type lingers in `lib/collections.ts` but no collection lists it and `/[collection]/badges` 307-redirects to `/overview` (badges render inline on edition/moment pages via `get_edition_badges_unified`).

Other top-level surfaces:
- **Global catalog search (site-wide, added 2026-08-11)** — `components/search/GlobalSearch.tsx`, mounted in `GlobalSiteHeader`, backed by public GET `/api/search`. It is the site's FIRST real search: everything that existed before searched ONE column with an unindexed `ilike` (`/api/edition-search` = `player_name` only / limit 10; `/api/search-editions` = auth-gated, for the alert-create modal) or was a client-side `.includes()` over already-loaded rows. Both old routes still exist and are NOT the global index — don't wire new callers to them.
- `/share/[wallet]` — shareable collection card with OG image
- `/profile/[username]` — public profile. **Reads through `lib/profile/public-profile.ts` (`getPublicProfile`, React-`cache()` memoized), NOT an HTTP self-fetch.** Carries the **Follow button** (`components/profile/FollowButton.tsx`, added 2026-08-11) — the follows table + CRUD route + dashboard friend-activity feed had existed since Phase 4 with **zero callers**, so follows sat at 0 rows and the feed showed its empty state permanently.
- **`/profile/[username]/trophy-case` — the shareable trophy case (added 2026-08-14)**, with its own card at `/api/og/trophy-case/[username]`. Until then the ONLY trophy-case export was a PDF, and **a PDF cannot unfurl** — pasting one into X or Discord produces a file, not a picture. It reuses `TrophySlab` in `public` mode rather than drawing its own tiles, so a visitor arriving from a shared link sees the case the owner actually arranged. Both it and `/profile/<u>` are **pinned public** in `__tests__/proxy-is-public-path.test.ts` (with `/profile/edit` pinned gated as the mirror) — ⚠ if the auth wall ever swallows one it does **not** 404: `proxy.ts` 302s to `/login` and the crawler gets an HTML login page **at status 200**, so every naive "did it respond?" check passes. That is exactly how `/fonts/*.ttf` stayed broken for weeks.
- ⚠ **The RPC handle now DEFAULTS from the Dapper/Top Shot username** (`lib/profile/username-from-topshot.ts` + `lib/profile/claim-username.ts`, 2026-08-14). **A username is what creates `/profile/<username>` at all**, and before this only **4 of 20** signed-up collectors had one — so 16 had no public profile to flair or share, and every social feature downstream was dead by default (backfilled: **4/20 → 20/20**). The claim is deliberately conservative: **never overwrite** an existing handle, **never steal** one (a `23505` returns `"taken"`, it does NOT auto-suffix), and **never throw** — a failed READ is not an absent handle. `RESERVED_HANDLES` includes `edit` and `settings` because those are REAL static segments that would beat `[username]` in the route table.
- `/analytics` and `/analytics/wallets/[address]` — analytics dashboards. Source lives under the `app/(analytics)/` route group (the group name doesn't affect the URL, so `find app/analytics` misses it). Sibling dashboards: `/analytics/{sales,loans,fmv,packs,sets,pulse,listings,methodology,api}`. Distinct from the per-collection `analytics` tab at `/[collection]/analytics`.
- `/admin/*` — internal tools incl. `/admin/flowty-analytics` (RPC_ADMIN_TOKEN gated)

Selected API endpoints worth knowing about:
- `/api/edition-stats`, `/api/pack-roi`, `/api/collection-snapshot`, `/api/overview-stats`
- `/api/admin/prune-pipeline-runs` (POST, Bearer `$INGEST_SECRET_TOKEN`; daily cron)
- `/api/wallet-backfill[-allday|-pinnacle|-golazos|-ufc|-multicollection]` — fire-and-forget Cadence walks; `?force=true` to bypass `skip_cached`
- `/api/seed-wallet-refresh` — orchestrator; cron-job.org still calls every 6h but an in-route gate (2026-07-18 cost lever) executes only the `utcHour % 12 < 2` waves (effective 12h cadence). `?force=1` bypasses (used by the GHA backstop — load-bearing, do not drop); env `SEED_WALLET_REFRESH_EVERY_WAVE=1` disables the gate
- **`/api/search`** (public GET, added 2026-08-11) — global catalog search over player / set / team / edition-key / play-type, via SECDEF service-role-only `rpc_search_catalog(p_q, p_collection_id, p_limit)`; `lib/search/href.ts` builds the result hrefs (in the primary coverage gate — a wrong href 404s on click). Three durable constraints: (a) it **MUST apply the platform canonical-edition predicate** — `editions` stores the same Top Shot moment under two key conventions (int `setID:playID` AND a UUID pair), so without it every TS hit returns twice and `edition_count` roughly doubles (Lillard read 95 vs the canonical 65); (b) it joins `AND c.is_active`, because unpublished collections (`candy_mlb`, `panini_blockchain`) have no `/[collection]/…` routes and their hits 404 on click; (c) on RPC failure it returns **503, never a 200 with `results: []`** — an empty array is byte-identical to a legitimate "nothing matched", so an outage would render as "we have no such moment" (the failure-renders-as-data class). ⚠ **SUPERSEDED 2026-08-13 — the catalog DOES hold descriptive prose now, and search reads it.** This entry used to state that narrative queries ("game winner", "buzzer beater") correctly return zero because no prose existed. That is no longer true: `editions.description` carries the paragraph the Top Shot moment page renders, and `rpc_search_catalog`'s edition arm searches it in both the index-backed anchor and the multi-token combined text, with a **0.12 `via_prose` ranking boost** so a deliberate narrative query surfaces the moments that ARE game-winners above editions that merely contain the words. ⚠ **DO NOT CITE `lillard game winner` → "Damian Lillard — For the Win" AS PROOF THIS WORKS — that was a FALSE POSITIVE and it is how the claim survived review.** "For the Win" is a SET NAME containing the query words, so that result is set-name matching mistaken for prose matching. Measured 2026-08-13: `game winner` returns a For-The-Win set roster (Blocks included) and misses the two most famous Blazers game winners (`48:1652` Archive, `121:4255` Run It Back: Legacies) though both carry descriptions. ⚠ **AND THE "OUTRANKED / length-normalized trigram" EXPLANATION THIS BULLET USED TO GIVE WAS ALSO WRONG — corrected 2026-08-14, on the third diagnosis.** The description is not in the similarity expression at all; a graded prose boost was built, measured, and **changed nothing**; and `ts_rank` reads 0.076 for nearly every row, correctly, because ~297 moments mention "buzzer" once and are genuinely equivalent. **The real defect was `LIKE ALL (v_pats)` — an AND over every query token** — so one word the prose never uses annihilated the query: `lillard buzzer beater` returned **zero rows** while `lillard buzzer` returned `121:4255` at rank 6. **Fixed** (`audit_20260814_rpc_search_catalog_token_coverage`): a **3-or-more-token query may miss ONE token**, ranked by a `(tok_hit / v_n) * 0.60` coverage term so a full match still outranks a partial one; a 1- or 2-token query must still match every word, because relaxing there degrades `lillard buzzer` into every Lillard moment. Validated as a separate prototype first — all three narrative failures fixed (`lillard buzzer beater` → rank 6, `damian lillard buzzer beater` → rank 6, `lillard game winner` → `48:1652` rank 20), all three working ranks and every entity query unmoved. ⚠ **The residual gap is STEMMING, and it is not fixable by switching to FTS**: the prose says "game-winning" and "buzzer", never "winner" or "beater", and Postgres English stemming does **not** relate winner↔winning or buzzer↔beater — `websearch_to_tsquery` also ANDs its terms by default, so the "proper" answer recommended twice in the filings would have failed on the exact two moments that motivated the work. **The reliable query shape is a NAME plus a distinctive word**, which is now stated in `/api/search`'s `meta.note` and the concierge prompt. See `docs/overnight/inbox/2026-08-14T0310Z-narrative-search-is-outranked-by-set-names.md` (title now historical). ⚠ **Coverage is PARTIAL and that partiality is disclosed, not hidden** — **9,128 of 13,208** canonical Top Shot editions (**69.1%**, measured 2026-08-14 — it was 44.6% at ship on 08-13 and the daily `topshot-catalog-backfill` cron moves it, so **any figure here is a dated sample, never a constant**) and **0% on every other collection** (All Day's ingest is WAF-blocked; nothing else has a prose source). So a narrative miss is AMBIGUOUS — "no such moment" vs "no description for that moment" — and `/api/search` reads the **`edition_description_coverage`** view LIVE into `meta.coverage` + `meta.note`, which `GlobalSearch`'s empty state renders. **NEVER hardcode the percentage**: the backfill moves it every run (the Panini lesson). A failed coverage read omits the disclosure rather than stating a number it cannot substantiate. Indexes: `idx_editions_team_name_trgm` (team lookup 162→33ms) and `idx_editions_description_trgm` (partial `WHERE description IS NOT NULL`; the whole call got FASTER, 33→23ms, because the extra selective index narrows earlier). Both built CONCURRENTLY out of band. ⚠ **It has a SECOND consumer as of 2026-08-13: the concierge's `search_catalog` tool.** So the `meta.coverage`/`meta.note` contract is now load-bearing for two surfaces — the header search panel renders it, and the bot is required by its system prompt to state which case an empty narrative result is. Changing or dropping those `meta` fields silently removes the assistant's only means of telling "we have no description for that" apart from "no such moment".

Collection registry: `lib/collections.ts` (8 collections defined; 5 currently published).
Old flat routes redirect to the new nested paths.

## Frequently used commands

```bash
# Development
npm run dev

# ⚠ A FRESH web/cloud sandbox clones with NO node_modules — run this FIRST.
# Without it `npx vitest` silently fetches its own vite and dies on
# `MODULE_NOT_FOUND ... vitest.config.ts`, which reads like a broken config
# rather than a missing install. `npx tsc` fails the same way.
npm ci

# TypeScript health check (use before deploying when Vercel rate-limited)
npx tsc --noEmit

# Tests (see "Testing & CI coverage" for details)
npm test                 # vitest run — route + lib unit/integration suites
npx vitest run __tests__/some-file.test.ts   # run a single test file
npm run test:coverage    # same suites + coverage ratchet (what CI gates on)
npm run test:cadence     # extract inline Cadence + `flow cadence lint` the fixtures
npm run test:cadence:escrow  # RPCTradeEscrow `flow test` suite (fetches deps first; NOT in CI)

# Git — always use Git Bash (MINGW64) on Windows
git status
git add -A && git commit -m "feat: ..."
git push origin main

# Vercel redeploy via REST (use PowerShell Invoke-WebRequest — curl fails silently in Git Bash)
# POST https://api.vercel.com/v13/deployments
# body: {"name":"rip-packs-city","gitSource":{"type":"github","repoId":"1188272071","ref":"main"}}

# Env var writes also require PowerShell Invoke-WebRequest
# POST https://api.vercel.com/v10/projects/{projectId}/env?teamId={teamId}

# Wallet backfill ad-hoc (force full re-walk)
# curl -X POST 'https://www.rippackscity.com/api/wallet-backfill?force=true' \
#   -H "Authorization: Bearer $INGEST_SECRET_TOKEN" \
#   -d '{"wallet":"0x..."}'
```
